Method: Proposes SAFAG, a symmetry annotation-free framework with (1) a stepwise refinement two-stage framework for candidate-to-final quaternion regression, and (2) symmetry prediction formulated as a probability distribution problem with self-supervised learning.

Result: Experimental results demonstrate superior performance and robustness compared to existing methods.

Conclusion: The work shows great potential for application in embodied AI systems requiring generalizable robot-object interaction.

Abstract: Urgently needed generalizable robot object interaction and manipulation requires high-quality Cross-Category object perception. As a pioneer of this area, Generalizable and Actionable Parts (GAParts) understanding has attracted increasing attention from relevant researchers. However, most recent works either have insufficient design regarding the symmetry issue or require rich symmetry annotation, which severely impedes precise GAPart pose estimation in data-lacking scenarios. In this paper, we propose SAFAG, a novel Symmetry Annotation-Free framework for Generalizable and Actionable Parts Pose Estimation. Specifically, we suggest a stepwise refinement two-stage framework for candidate-to-final quaternion regression, and tackle the symmetry prediction as a probability distribution problem with self-supervised learning strategy. The experimental results demonstrate the superior performance and robustness of our SAFAG. We believe that our work has the enormous potential to be applied in many areas of embodied AI system.

</details>


### [155] [How to Instruct Your Robot: Dense Language Annotations Power Robot Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17077)
*Bosung Kim,Ruiyi Wang,David Acuna,Jaehun Jung,Alexander Trevithick,Brandon Cui,Yejin Choi,Prithviraj Ammanabrolu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: DeMiAn (Dense Multi-aspect Annotation) improves robot policy learning by re-annotating existing demonstration videos with VLM-generated dense language descriptions across four aspects (motion, scene, arm pose, reasoning), then using a learned instructor to select task-appropriate annotations at deployment, boosting policy performance without collecting new demonstrations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robot policy learning is bottlenecked by the high cost of collecting demonstrations, while language annotations for existing data are comparatively cheap. The paper aims to extract more learning signal from fixed robot/egocentric video corpora through dense language re-annotation.

Method: Two-stage approach: (1) Re-label demonstration segments with VLM-generated annotations along four complementary aspects (physical motion, scene composition, arm pose, reasoning); (2) A learned instructor maps task description + initial scene snapshot to a task-appropriate annotation at deployment, running asynchronously to hide latency behind policy execution.

Result: Across 1M robot clips and 50K EgoVerse videos, DeMiAn improves both VLA policies and video-based world-action models. On RoboCasa, instructor raises success by 5 points over task-only baseline, within 3 points of per-task oracle. No single annotation aspect dominates across tasks. Improves composite-task and OOD performance, shifts compute-performance frontier.

Conclusion: Dense re-annotation is a practical scaling lever for robot policy learning, extracting more signal from fixed datasets without collecting new demonstrations.

Abstract: Scaling robot policy learning is bottlenecked by the cost of collecting demonstrations, while language annotations for existing demonstrations are comparatively cheap. We study language density as a lever for extracting more signal from a fixed robot or egocentric-video corpus. We introduce DeMiAn (Dense Multi-aspect Annotation), a two-stage approach that first re-labels demonstration segments with VLM-generated annotations along four complementary aspects: physical motion, scene composition, arm pose, and reasoning. A learned instructor then maps a task description and initial scene snapshot to a task-appropriate annotation at deployment, running asynchronously so generation latency is hidden behind policy execution. Across over 1M robot manipulation clips and 50K EgoVerse human-egocentric videos, DeMiAn improves both a vision-language-action policy and a video-based world-action model without collecting new demonstrations. On RoboCasa, the instructor raises success by 5 points over a task-only baseline and comes within 3 points of a per-task oracle. No fixed annotation aspect dominates across tasks, showing that selecting the right dense language matters. DeMiAn also improves composite-task and out-of-distribution performance, and shifts the compute-performance frontier in both mid-training and post-training after accounting for annotation-generation FLOPs. These results position dense re-annotation as a practical scaling lever for robot policy learning.

</details>


### [156] [Contrastive Conceptor Activation Steering (COAST): Unlocking Vision-Language-Action Models through Hidden States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17144)
*Miranda Muqing Miao,Subin Kim,Brandon Yang,Lyle Ungar*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: COAST is a lightweight, training-free method that uses conceptors to steer VLA model latent representations toward success-critical subspaces, improving task success rates by over 20% (simulation) and 40% (real-robot) across diverse policy architectures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, despite leveraging powerful VLM pre-training priors, remain brittle and frequently fail at simple robotic tasks, indicating a decoding bottleneck that prevents full utilization of task-relevant knowledge in latent representations.

Method: COAST builds on conceptors (linear operators that soft-project data into principal components of a target distribution). It identifies success-critical subspaces from a few success/failure rollout examples, then at inference time steers VLA latent representations into those subspaces to improve task outcomes.

Result: Across three architecturally distinct policies (flow-matching VLA, autoregressive VLA, Diffusion Policy), COAST improves absolute mean simulation and real-robot task success rates by over 20% and 40% respectively. Success representations are largely task-specific, while failure modes share substantial structure across tasks.

Conclusion: Current VLAs retain substantial task-relevant knowledge in latent representations, and the action expert's decoding bottleneck can be mitigated by steering the residual stream toward task-relevant subspaces. COAST offers a training-free path to unlocking latent capabilities by steering models toward their own "success" distributions.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models leverage powerful perceptual priors from web-scale Vision-Language Model (VLM) pre-training, yet they remain surprisingly brittle in practice, frequently failing at simple robotic tasks. To mitigate this, we propose Contrastive Conceptor Activation Steering (COAST). COAST builds on the notion of a "conceptor", a linear operator that soft-projects data into the principal components of a target distribution. COAST uses conceptors to identify success-critical subspaces for a target robotic task from a few examples of success and failure rollouts. At inference time, it steers VLA latents into these identified success subspaces to improve task outcomes. Across three architecturally distinct neural policies (flow-matching VLA, autoregressive VLA, and Diffusion Policy), COAST improves absolute mean simulation and real-robot task success rate by over 20 and 40% respectively. The activation subspace geometry reveals that failure modes share substantial structure across tasks while success representations remain largely task-specific. When tasks share similar failure modes, this structure enables previously fitted conceptors to improve performance on new tasks without refitting. Ultimately, our results suggest that current VLAs retain substantial task-relevant knowledge in their latent representations, and that the action expert's decoding bottleneck could be mitigated by steering its residual stream toward task-relevant subspaces. COAST provides a lightweight, training-free path to unlocking these latent capabilities by steering the model towards its own "success" distributions.

</details>


### [157] [Event-Grounded Sparse Autoencoders for Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17204)
*Xinchen Jin,Aditya Chatterjee,Pranav Kumar,Rohan Paleja*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes an event-grounded interpretability pipeline for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies, anchoring SAE feature analysis to behavioral events (keyframes) rather than text contexts, demonstrating causal effects on OpenVLA and π0.5 across simulations and real-robot studies, while highlighting SAE's limitations as an intervention basis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mechanistic interpretability tools from language and vision-language models do not transfer cleanly to VLAs because outputs are robot actions (not tokens) and interventions require expensive closed-loop rollouts. Existing SAE analysis lacks behavioral grounding in VLA contexts.

Method: End-effector keyframes are clustered using visual, state, and temporal cues within each task. SAE features are linked to these behaviorally salient events, with optional VLM annotations for semantic context. The pipeline grounds SAE-based VLA analysis in closed-loop behavioral events rather than text contexts.

Result: Event-grounded ranking yields the strongest causal effects on OpenVLA and transfers to continuous action chunks of π0.5. SAE is a sparse but imperfect intervention basis, with usability varying across architectures and intervention sites. Aggressive intervention reveals safety and interpretability limits.

Conclusion: Event-grounded SAE analysis is a practical starting point for behavior-anchored VLA interpretability, motivating future work on SAE features beyond action-aligned coordinates, finer-grained closed-loop evaluation, and safe interventions for high-stakes VLA deployments.

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies translate language and visual inputs into robot actions, where their hidden representations directly shape closed-loop behavior. However, mechanistic interpretability tools from language and vision-language models do not transfer cleanly to VLAs: outputs are robot actions rather than human-readable tokens, and interventions can only be tested via expensive closed-loop rollouts. We propose an event-grounded interpretability pipeline that anchors SAE feature analysis to behavioral events rather than text contexts. End-effector keyframes are clustered within each task using visual, state, and temporal cues, linking SAE features to behaviorally salient events and, via optional VLM annotations, to semantic context. To our knowledge, our pipeline is among the first to ground SAE-based VLA analysis in closed-loop behavioral events. Across two simulation architectures and a real-robot study, event-grounded ranking yields the strongest causal effects on OpenVLA and transfers to the continuous action chunks of $π_{0.5}$. SAE is a sparse but imperfect intervention basis: usability varies with architecture and intervention site, and aggressive intervention reveals safety and interpretability limits. Overall, event-grounded SAE analysis emerges as a practical starting point for behavior-anchored VLA interpretability, motivating future work on SAE features beyond action-aligned coordinates, finer-grained closed-loop evaluation, and safe interventions for high-stakes VLA deployments. Code is available at \url{https://github.com/xc-j/Event-SAE}.

</details>


### [158] [Generating Realistic Safety-Critical Scenarios for Vehicle-Pedestrian Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17229)
*Qingwen Pu,Kun Xie,Yuan Zhu,Guocong Zhai*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A three-stage framework combining real-world data and adaptive simulation (MA-SST-DDPG agents) generates 198,000+ realistic safety-critical vehicle-pedestrian interaction episodes, achieving behavior indistinguishable from real-world data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world datasets rarely capture high-risk vehicle-pedestrian scenarios, and simulation platforms lack realistic behavior, limiting rigorous validation of automated driving systems.

Method: (1) Pre-train MA-SST-DDPG agents on real-world safety-critical data; (2) deploy in CARLA for online RL to generalize; (3) generate VPSCI dataset (198K episodes) from eight intersection scenarios.

Result: Refined MA-SST-DDPG achieved ADE=0.072m, FDE=0.142m; distributional equivalence to real-world data in conflict severity and behavior; Turing test confirmed indistinguishability.

Conclusion: The framework effectively produces high-fidelity safety-critical data, supporting ADS validation and simulation-based safety evaluations.

Abstract: Automated driving system deployment requires rigorous validation across safety-critical vehicle-pedestrian interactions, yet real-world datasets rarely capture high-risk scenarios while simulation platforms lack realistic behavior. In response, this study proposes a three-stage framework that combines real-world grounding with adaptive simulation to generate behaviorally realistic safety-critical scenarios at scale. Stage 1 pre-trains multi-agent state-space Transformer-enhanced DDPG (MA-SST-DDPG) agents on real-world safety-critical data to learn human-like interactive evasive behaviors through data-driven learning. Stage 2 deploys pre-trained multi-agents in CARLA for online reinforcement learning to generalize across diverse scenarios, integrating real-world knowledge with simulation experience to produce a refined MA-SST-DDPG model. Stage 3 uses CARLA with the refined model to generate over 198,000 high-resolution interaction episodes from eight intersection scenarios, culminating in the Vehicle-Pedestrian Safety-Critical Interaction (VPSCI) dataset. The Refined MA-SST-DDPG model outperformed baseline methods in reproducing realistic evasive behaviors, achieving the lowest trajectory errors (ADE = 0.072 m, FDE = 0.142 m). Statistical comparison confirmed distributional equivalence between the generated and real-world data in both conflict severity and behavioral response. A Turing test confirmed that the three-stage framework generated evasive behaviors were indistinguishable from real-world interactions. These results demonstrate the framework's effectiveness in producing high-fidelity safety-critical data, offering valuable sources for the development of ADS and simulation-based safety evaluations.

</details>


### [159] [SEDualVLN: A Spatially-Enhanced Dual-System for Vision-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17249)
*Jingzhi Huang,Junkai Huang,Wenxuan Song,Haoyang Yang,Hailong Huang,Haoang Li,Yi Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: SEDualVLN proposes a dual-system framework combining a spatially-enhanced VLM (System 1) for fast action generation and an MLLM with 3D mapping (System 2) for slow waypoint planning, achieving state-of-the-art results on VLN-CE benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLN approaches fall into two paradigms: end-to-end VLM policies that struggle with long-horizon tasks and lack dynamic reasoning, and zero-shot modular pipelines that suffer from limited spatial grounding and high reasoning time. The paper aims to bridge this gap by combining fast action generation with slow deliberative planning.

Method: SEDualVLN consists of two systems: System 1 uses a VLM enhanced with global and local spatial awareness for direct action generation; System 2 integrates a general MLLM with a mapping module that plans waypoints using top-down 3D map views and rendered path images. The two systems cooperate via a fast-slow coordinated approach.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on VLN-CE benchmarks. Ablation studies confirm the effectiveness of each system and module.

Conclusion: The dual-system design effectively addresses the limitations of both end-to-end and zero-shot VLN paradigms by leveraging complementary spatial enhancements and a fast-slow coordination mechanism.

Abstract: Vision-Language Navigation (VLN) approaches have currently followed two primary paradigms: the end-to-end Vision-Language Model (VLM) policy fine-tuned on navigation trajectories to directly predict actions, and the zero-shot modular pipeline integrating pre-trained Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) for training-free generalization to unseen environments. However, end-to-end methods struggle with long-horizon navigation and lack dynamic reasoning, whereas zero-shot methods are constrained by limited spatial grounding for reliable planning and also require substantial reasoning time. To bridge this gap, we introduce SEDualVLN, a spatially-enhanced dual-system VLN framework. System 1 is a VLM model enhanced with both global and local spatial awareness, used for action generation. System 2 integrates a general MLLM with a mapping module, wherein the MLLM plans waypoints by leveraging top-down views of the real-time 3D map alongside streams of rendered path images. Both systems leverage different forms of spatial enhancement to cultivate the agent's sense of direction in VLN tasks. Ultimately, they cooperate to complete the navigation task through a fast-slow coordinated approach. SEDualVLN achieves state-of-the-art performance on VLN-CE benchmarks, and further ablation studies demonstrate the effectiveness of each system and module.

</details>


### [160] [Stretch-ICP: A Continuous-Trajectory Registration and Deskewing Algorithm in Scenarios of Aggressive Motions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17264)
*Simon-Pierre Deschênes,Veronica Vannini,Philippe Giguère,François Pomerleau*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper introduces the TIGS dataset (lidar/IMU data from a sensor tumbling downhill with extreme angular velocities) and proposes two methods—SAAVE (saturation-aware gyroscope estimation) and Stretch-ICP (improved registration/deskewing)—that together significantly reduce angular and linear velocity estimation errors (up to 95.2% and 94.8%) under aggressive motions, enhancing lidar-inertial SLAM robustness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robust autonomy in complex environments is challenged by terrain-induced instability (uneven/slippery surfaces) that causes extreme accelerations and angular velocities, corrupting sensor measurements and degrading state estimation. Existing methods lack robustness under such aggressive motions.

Method: 1) Introduced the TIGS dataset (mechanical lidar + IMU recordings from a sensor tumbling downhill). 2) Proposed SAAVE to estimate angular velocities when gyroscope measurements saturate. 3) Proposed Stretch-ICP, a novel registration and deskewing algorithm for smoother 6-DOF trajectory reconstruction under aggressive motions.

Result: SAAVE reduced angular speed estimation error by 83.4%. Stretch-ICP reduced linear and angular velocity errors by 95.2% and 94.8%, respectively, at scan boundaries.

Conclusion: The combined contributions (TIGS dataset, SAAVE, Stretch-ICP) improve the robustness and consistency of lidar-inertial state estimation under aggressive motions.

Abstract: Robust robotic autonomy remains challenging in complex environments, where loss of stability on uneven or slippery terrain can induce extreme accelerations and angular velocities. Such motions corrupt sensor measurements and degrade state estimation, motivating the need for improved algorithmic robustness. To investigate this issue, we introduce the Tumbling-Induced Gyroscope Saturation (TIGS) dataset, which consists of recordings from a mechanical lidar and an Inertial Measurement Unit (IMU) tumbling down a hill. The dataset contains angular speeds up to four times higher than those in similar datasets and is publicly available. We then propose two complementary methods to improve Simultaneous Localization And Mapping (SLAM) robustness and evaluate them on TIGS. First, Saturation-Aware Angular Velocity Estimation (SAAVE) estimates angular velocities when gyroscope measurements become saturated during aggressive motions, reducing angular speed estimation error by 83.4%. Second, Stretch-ICP, a novel registration and deskewing algorithm, enables reconstruction of smoother 6-Degrees Of Freedom (DOF) trajectories under aggressive motions compared to classical Iterative Closest Point (ICP). Stretch-ICP reduces linear and angular velocity errors by 95.2% and 94.8%, respectively, at scan boundaries. Together, these contributions improve the robustness and consistency of lidar-inertial state estimation under aggressive motions.

</details>


### [161] [Task Capability Improvement Algorithm for Collaborative Manipulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17293)
*Keshab Patra,Arpita Sinha,Anirban Guha*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes using undesired moments (generated when forces are applied away from the object's center of gravity) to improve cooperative manipulation task capability, achieving a 5.86% improvement in simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance cooperative manipulation capability by leveraging additional moments that naturally arise when forces are applied at grasp points away from the object's center of gravity, rather than treating them as disturbances.

Method: The manipulators apply forces at the object's grasp point. Forces applied away from the center of gravity create undesired moments, which are treated as additional moments to improve individual and group manipulation capability.

Result: Simulation results demonstrate a 5.86% improvement in task capability when utilizing moments compared to scenarios without moment-based enhancement.

Conclusion: Additional moments from off-center force application can be exploited to enhance cooperative manipulation capability, leading to optimal capability, resource allocation, and fault tolerance.

Abstract: This work introduces a cooperative task capability improvement utilizing additional moments. The manipulators apply forces at the object's grasp point. Applying forces at a point other than the object's center of gravity produces undesired moments. The undesired moment acts as an additional moment. It improves the capability of an individual manipulator and, hence, the entire collaborative group. Any improvements in task capability directly add up to the object and transportation capability. The group's enhanced capability also helps achieve optimal capability, optimal resource allocation, and maximum fault tolerance in object manipulation. Our simulation results show an improvement in the capability of 5.86 \% compared to when no moment is used to enhance the capability of the manipulators.

</details>


### [162] [HCLM: A Hierarchical Framework for Cooperative Loco-Manipulation with Dual Quadrupeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17300)
*Qixuan Li,Chen Le,Jincheng Yu,Xinlei Chen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: HCLM is a hierarchical framework for cooperative loco-manipulation with dual quadrupedal robots, decoupling high-level collaborative reasoning (via Joint Diffusion Policy with SE(3)-invariant representations) from low-level motion execution (via Whole-Body Controller combining proactive MPC and reactive admittance control), validated in simulation and real-world tasks like carrying, packing, and handovers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coordinating multi-robot collaborative manipulation across floating bases is highly challenging due to conflicting demands of spatial coordination, robust locomotion, and closed-chain physical interactions.

Method: A centralized Joint Diffusion Policy with SE(3)-invariant task-space representation for high-level collaborative reasoning, and a task-centric hybrid Whole-Body Controller combining proactive kinematic Model Predictive Control with a reactive execution layer featuring cooperative admittance control for force regulation and kinematic conflict resolution.

Result: Demonstrated reliable task execution, strict configuration agnosticism, and exceptional resilience against severe physical perturbations in both simulated (carrying, packing, handovers) and real-world (handovers) scenarios.

Conclusion: The framework offers a highly robust pathway for multi-robot embodied coordination through systematic decoupling of high-level reasoning and low-level control.

Abstract: We introduce HCLM, a hierarchical framework for general-purpose cooperative loco-manipulation with dual quadrupedal systems. Coordinating multi-robot collaborative manipulation across floating bases is highly challenging due to the conflicting demands of spatial coordination, robust locomotion, and closed-chain physical interactions. To resolve this, our architecture systematically decouples high-level collaborative reasoning from low-level robust motion execution. At the high level, a centralized Joint Diffusion Policy leverages an SE(3)-invariant task-space representation to learn coordinate-agnostic spatial coordination patterns. To translate these frame-agnostic references into physical motion, a task-centric hybrid Whole-Body Controller synergizes a proactive kinematic Model Predictive Control for collision-free velocity distribution with a reactive execution layer. Crucially, this reactive layer guarantees rapid responsiveness for precise end-effector tracking, while concurrently integrating active force regulation via a cooperative admittance scheme to safely resolve kinematic conflicts and strictly regulate internal stresses during closed-chain interactions. We validate the framework across progressively challenging simulated scenarios, including cooperative carrying, packing and handovers, and successfully deploy the latter in the real world. The results demonstrate reliable task execution, strict configuration agnosticism, and exceptional resilience against severe physical perturbations, offering a highly robust pathway for multi-robot embodied coordination.

</details>


### [163] [Beyond Geometry: Efficient Topologically-Grounded Navigation in Complex 3D Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17302)
*Yifan Du,Chengwei Zhang,Siyu Liao,Zhongfeng Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A surface extraction framework for ground robot navigation reduces state space by over 80% via ground support, overhead clearance, and connectivity constraints, achieving sub-millisecond A* search and 100% planning success across 300 queries in complex 3D environments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Complex 3D environments cause geometric ambiguity (e.g., furniture vs. navigable ground) and high computational cost from massive voxel space searching, hindering ground robot navigation.

Method: A surface extraction framework that constructs a reduced state space of physically reachable standing positions using three constraints: ground support, overhead clearance, and seed-based connectivity.

Result: Over 80% state space reduction, sub-millisecond A* search on Matterport3D scenes, and 100% planning success across all 300 tested queries on Matterport3D and PCT benchmark scenes.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively addresses geometric ambiguity and computational challenges in 3D ground robot navigation by drastically reducing state space while maintaining perfect planning success.

Abstract: Ground robot navigation in complex 3D environments is often hindered by geometric ambiguity, where non-traversable structures such as furniture share local geometric properties with navigable ground. Furthermore, the computational cost of searching massive voxel spaces remains a significant challenge. To address these issues, we present a surface extraction framework that constructs a reduced state space of physically reachable standing positions by enforcing ground support, overhead clearance, and seed-based connectivity constraints. Evaluation across five Matterport3D indoor scenes and three PCT benchmark scenes demonstrates over 80\% state space reduction and sub-millisecond A* search on the Matterport3D scenes, with 100\% planning success across all 300 tested queries.

</details>


### [164] [Efficient Feature-Free Initialization for Monocular Visual-Inertial Systems Using a Feed-Forward 3D Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17327)
*Yuantai Zhang,Jiaqi Yang,Huajian Zeng,Changhao Chen,Haoang Li,Liang Li,Dezhen Song,Xingxing Zuo*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper proposes a feature-free visual-inertial initialization framework that uses feed-forward 3D models to predict up-to-scale point clouds directly from images, eliminating the need for visual feature tracking. This method achieves >90% initialization success rate, reduces required data duration to under 1.2 seconds, and performs robustly in visually degraded environments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VINS initialization methods heavily rely on visual feature correspondences and require 3-4 seconds of sensory data, which limits their applicability and efficiency, especially in visually degraded environments.

Method: A feature-free initialization framework that leverages up-to-scale point clouds predicted by a feed-forward 3D model, obviating the need for visual feature tracking and estimation.

Result: Achieves the highest success rate exceeding 90%, reduces required data duration to under 1.2 seconds, and demonstrates robust performance in visually degraded environments on public and self-collected datasets.

Conclusion: The proposed feature-free approach substantially reduces system complexity and improves initialization reliability, outperforming existing methods particularly in challenging conditions.

Abstract: Fast and reliable initialization is critical for monocular visual-inertial navigation systems (VINS), as it establishes the starting conditions for subsequent state estimation. Despite steady progress, most existing methods heavily rely on visual feature correspondences and require 3-4 seconds of sensory data for successful initialization, which limits their applicability and efficiency. With the advent of feed-forward 3D models that can directly predict point clouds from images, we revisit the visual-inertial initialization problem from a concise perspective. In this work, we propose a feature-free initialization framework that leverages up-to-scale point clouds predicted by a feed-forward 3D model, thereby obviating the need for visual feature tracking and estimation. This design substantially reduces system complexity and improves the reliability of initialization. Experiments on public datasets demonstrate that the proposed feature-free initialization method achieves the highest success rate, exceeding 90%, and significantly reduces the data duration required for successful initialization, typically to under 1.2 s. We further validate our method on a self-collected dataset covering various indoor and outdoor scenarios, demonstrating robust performance, particularly in visually degraded environments where existing methods often fail. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/Yuantai-Z/FF-VIO-Init.

</details>


### [165] [Tactile-based Multimodal Fusion in Embodied Intelligence: A Survey of Vision, Language, and Contact-Driven Paradigms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17336)
*Zhixiang Cao,Di Tian,Runwei Guan,Yanzhou Mu,Xiaolou Sun,Shaofeng Liang,Daizong Liu,Tao Huang,Yutao Yue,Henghui Ding,Bin Fang,Alex Zhou,Qing-Long Han,Hui Xiong*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper provides a comprehensive survey of multimodal tactile fusion research up to Q1 2026, proposing a hierarchical taxonomy covering tactile-vision-language datasets and methods across perception, cross-modal generation, and interaction, while summarizing hardware, benchmarks, and future directions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tactile sensing is essential for embodied intelligence but unimodal tactile perception is limited by sparse spatial coverage and lack of semantic context. Existing research is fragmented across datasets, modalities, and tasks without a unified theoretical framework, motivating the need for a comprehensive survey.

Method: The paper proposes a hierarchical taxonomy organizing multimodal tactile fusion into two dimensions: (1) multimodal datasets (Tactile-Vision, Tactile-Language, Tactile-Vision-Language, Tactile-Vision-Other), and (2) multimodal methods with three pillars: Multimodal Perception and Recognition, Cross-Modal Generation, and Multimodal Interaction.

Result: The survey categorizes tactile sensing hardware, datasets, and methods; reviews evaluation metrics and benchmarks; and identifies current challenges and future directions in the field up to early 2026.

Conclusion: Integrating tactile sensing with vision and language via multimodal fusion bridges physical interaction and semantic reasoning, but the field needs unified frameworks; this survey organizes existing work and highlights promising directions for future research.

Abstract: Tactile sensing is a fundamental modality for embodied intelligence, offering unique and direct feedback on contact geometry, material properties, and interaction dynamics that remote sensors cannot replace. However, unimodal tactile perception is inherently limited by its sparse spatial coverage and lack of global semantic context. With the recent explosion in deep learning and large language models, integrating tactile with vision and language has become essential to bridge physical interaction with semantic reasoning, leading to the emergence of Multimodal Tactile Fusion. Despite rapid progress, the existing researches remain fragmented across disparate datasets, sensing modalities, and tasks, lacking a unified theoretical framework. To address this gap, this paper provides a comprehensive survey of multimodal tactile fusion research up to the first quarter of 2026. We propose a hierarchical taxonomy that organizes the field into two primary dimensions: multimodal datasets and multimodal methods. On the data side, we categorize resources ranging from Tactile-Vision datasets, Tactile-Language datasets, Tactile-Vision-Language datasets, and Tactile-Vision-Other datasets. On the method side, we structure prior work into three core pillars: (1) Multimodal Perception and Recognition, which focuses on object understanding and grasp prediction; (2) Cross-Modal Generation, focusing on bidirectional translation between tactile, vision, and text; and (3) Multimodal Interaction, emphasizing feedback control and language-guided manipulation. Furthermore, we summarize representative tactile sensing hardware, review commonly used evaluation metrics and benchmark settings, and discuss current challenges and promising future directions.

</details>


### [166] [MUSE: Multimodal Uncertainty Quantification of State Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17421)
*Minkyung Kim,Henry Che,Bhargav Chandaka,Bhumsitt Pramuanpornsatid,Chengyu Yang,Sheng Cheng,Xiaofeng Wang,Naira Hovakimyan,Shenlong Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: MUSE is a real-time learning-based framework using Mamba for accurate uncertainty quantification in visual-inertial odometry, handling heteroscedastic and multimodal estimation challenges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite advances in visual state estimation accuracy, quantifying and calibrating estimation precision (confidence and failure detection) remains a fundamental challenge, especially in VIO where the problem is heteroscedastic and multimodal.

Method: MUSE uses Mamba's sequential modeling capacity to estimate localization uncertainty from multiple asynchronous sensor streams in real-time.

Result: Superior reliability and robustness compared to existing uncertainty quantification methods on public and in-house datasets; ablation studies confirm benefits of key design choices.

Conclusion: The proposed learning-based framework effectively addresses the heteroscedastic and multimodal uncertainty quantification challenge in VIO, offering real-time, reliable uncertainty estimates for state estimation.

Abstract: Accurate visual state estimation has been a central topic in robotics with a wide range of applications in robot navigation, autonomous driving, and autonomous flight. Recent advances in robot perception have led to significant improvements in the accuracy and robustness of state estimation, yet a fundamental challenge remains in how to quantify and calibrate its precision, i.e., how confident we are in an estimate and whether failures can be detected. This issue is particularly pronounced in visual-inertial odometry (VIO), where the heteroscedastic and multimodal nature of the problem makes uncertainty quantification especially difficult. This paper introduces MUSE (Multimodal Uncertainty Quantification of State Estimation), a novel real-time learning-based framework that leverages the strong and efficient sequential modeling capacity of Mamba to estimate localization uncertainty from multiple asynchronous sensor streams. Experiments on both public and in-house datasets demonstrate that MUSE achieves superior reliability and robustness compared to existing uncertainty quantification methods, and ablation studies justify the benefits of its key design choices.

</details>


### [167] [Rapid Vibration Suppression and Trajectory Tracking of a Serial Manipulator with Multi-Flexible Links](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17477)
*Chengyi Wang,Yilong Huang,Ji Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper proposes a backstepping output-feedback control framework with DeepONet approximation for fast vibration suppression and tip tracking of n-degree-of-freedom serial flexible manipulator robots, modeled as Timoshenko beams coupled with ODEs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Flexible robotic manipulators suffer from structural flexibility that induces vibrations, accelerates fatigue, degrades tracking performance, and limits operational speed, especially in multi-link serial configurations.

Method: Each link-joint is modeled as a Timoshenko beam coupled with an ODE, transformed into a canonical hyperbolic PDE with boundary dynamics. A backstepping-based boundary controller at the joint injects distributed damping along the beam. A DeepONet neural operator approximates backstepping kernels for real-time implementation and scalability.

Result: Experiments on a two-link flexible manipulator demonstrate faster vibration suppression and convergence of the end-effector to the desired trajectory compared with a linear quadratic regulator (LQR) with feedforward control.

Conclusion: The proposed backstepping output-feedback framework with DeepONet-based kernel approximation enables fast vibration suppression and trajectory tracking for multi-link flexible manipulators, with practical real-time deployability and scalability.

Abstract: Flexible robotic manipulators (FRMs) offer advantages in lightweight design and large workspace, but their structural flexibility induces vibrations, accelerates fatigue, degrades tracking performance, and limits operational speed. These challenges are further amplified in multi-link serial manipulators, where increased overall length leads to greater structural flexibility. This article presents a backstepping output-feedback framework for fast vibration suppression and tip tracking of an n-degree-of-freedom serial flexible manipulator robot (nDSFMR), with a DeepONet-based approximation for practical deployment. Each link-joint is modeled as a Timoshenko beam coupled with an ODE and transformed into a canonical hyperbolic PDE with boundary dynamics. A backstepping-based boundary controller at the joint is developed to equivalently inject distributed damping along the beam, enabling rapid vibration suppression and trajectory tracking, only using available boundary measurements. To enable real-time implementation and scalability, a DeepONet neural operator is introduced to approximate the backstepping kernels, significantly reducing computational cost and facilitating fast controller updates under varying operating conditions. Experiments on a two-link flexible manipulator demonstrate faster vibration suppression and convergence of the end-effector to the desired trajectory, compared with a linear quadratic regulator (LQR) with feedforward control.

</details>


### [168] [DyGRO-VLA: Cross-Task Scaling of Vision-Language-Action Models via Dynamic Grouped Residual Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17486)
*Sixu Lin,Yunpeng Qing,Litao Liu,Ming Zhou,Ruixing Jin,Xiaoyi Fan,Guiliang Liu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: DyGRO-VLA is a two-stage RL optimization framework for VLA models that captures cross-task latent representations via information-theoretic principles and dynamically refines policy optimization using mixture-of-RL-residuals, improving multi-task generalization on LIBERO, RoboTwin2, and real-world benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current RL optimizers for VLA models are task-specific, causing overfitting to narrow task sets and reducing generalist controllers to specialized policies. The paper aims to address the loss of cross-task generalizability in RL-optimized VLA models.

Method: A two-stage optimization framework: Stage 1 captures cross-task latent representations using information-theoretic principles; Stage 2 dynamically refines policy optimization via a mixture-of-RL-residuals, exploiting task-relevant latent information while mitigating adverse interference on learned representations.

Result: Consistent improvements over strong baselines on LIBERO, RoboTwin2 benchmarks, and real-world validation under multi-task training and distribution shift.

Conclusion: Cross-task feature representations are critical for VLA model generalizability, and the proposed DyGRO-VLA framework effectively preserves these representations during RL optimization, enabling generalist multi-task control.

Abstract: Recent progress in Reinforcement Learning (RL) provides a principled approach to optimizing Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, facilitating a shift from trajectory imitation to active learning in the task environment. Despite improvements in control precision, most RL optimizers remain task-specific, which reduces VLA models from generalist controllers to policies that overfit to a narrow set of tasks. In this study, we conduct an in-depth analysis of this phenomenon and highlight the importance of cross-task feature representations for improving the generalizability of VLA models. Motivated by this finding, we introduce DyGRO-VLA, a two-stage optimization framework that 1) effectively captures cross-task latent representations based on information-theoretic principles, and 2) dynamically refines policy optimization via a mixture-of-RL-residuals. DyGRO-VLA enables the RL optimizer to exploit task-relevant latent information while strategically mitigating adverse interference on the learned representations throughout the optimization process. We evaluate our approach on LIBERO, RoboTwin2 benchmarks, and further validate it on real world, demonstrating consistent improvements over strong baselines under multi-task training and distribution shift.

</details>


### [169] [AffordVLA: Injecting Affordance Representations into Vision-Language-Action Models via Implicit Feature Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17517)
*Weijie Kong,Zhian Su,Wei Yu,Huixu Dong*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: AffordVLA enhances VLA robotic models by internalizing affordance perception (task-relevant functional interaction regions) into visual representations via implicit representation alignment with a zero-shot affordance teacher, improving manipulation success without extra annotations or inference overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLA models are dominated by global object appearance and struggle to focus on task-relevant functional interaction regions; affordance methods rely on explicit mask injection or external modules requiring extra annotations and causing errors/overhead.

Method: Constructs a zero-shot affordance teacher to extract task-conditioned affordance visual representations from RGB and language instructions, then aligns intermediate VLA visual representations with these affordance representations implicitly via representation alignment.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance in simulation and real-world experiments, outperforming strong baselines; ablation shows improved visual representation quality, manipulation success rates, and training efficiency while preserving inference efficiency.

Conclusion: Internalizing affordance perception through implicit alignment effectively enhances VLA visual representations for manipulation tasks without additional annotation or inference overhead.

Abstract: Recent advances in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have shown strong potential for general-purpose robotic manipulation. However, the visual representations of most VLA models are often dominated by global object appearance and struggle to focus on task-relevant functional interaction regions, which limits their robustness in unstructured environments. Existing affordance-based methods typically rely on explicit mask injection or external perception modules, requiring additional annotations while introducing cascading perception errors and inference overhead. To address these limitations, we propose AffordVLA, an affordance-enhanced VLA framework that internalizes manipulation-centric affordance perception into VLA visual representations through implicit representation alignment. Specifically, we construct a zero-shot affordance teacher to extract task-conditioned affordance visual representations from RGB observations and language instructions. AffordVLA aligns the intermediate visual representations of the VLA with the affordance visual representations extracted by the teacher, thereby implicitly injecting manipulation-centric affordance perception into VLA visual representations and improving action accuracy. Extensive simulation and real-world experiments demonstrate that AffordVLA and its affordance teacher achieve state-of-the-art performance and outperform strong baselines. Ablation analyses show that AffordVLA effectively reshapes VLA visual representations while preserving inference efficiency, leading to improved manipulation success rates and training efficiency.

</details>


### [170] [RoboFlow4D: A Lightweight Flow World Model Toward Real-Time Flow-Guided Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17522)
*Sixu Lin,Junliang Chen,Huaiyuan Xu,Zhuohao Li,Guangming Wang,Yixiong Jing,Sheng Xu,Runyi Zhao,Brian Sheil,Lap-Pui Chau,Guiliang Liu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RoboFlow4D is a lightweight end-to-end flow world model that unifies perception and planning by predicting multi-frame 3D flows from visual observations and text instructions, enabling real-time, resource-efficient robotic manipulation through a slow-fast collaboration between flow prediction and action control, achieving improved success rates and efficiency in both simulation and real-world settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing predictive flow planners for 3D robotic manipulation rely on modular pipelines with multiple submodels, leading to high computational overhead and limited real-time performance, which hinders efficient embodied manipulation.

Method: RoboFlow4D is an end-to-end framework that directly predicts multi-frame 3D flows from visual observations and textual instructions, forming an observation-planning-execution closed loop with slow-fast collaboration between flow prediction and action control, seamlessly integrating with general action policies.

Result: Extensive experiments in both simulation and real-world settings demonstrate that RoboFlow4D consistently improves manipulation success rates and computational efficiency over existing approaches.

Conclusion: RoboFlow4D advances flow-guided planning for embodied intelligence by enabling real-time, resource-efficient manipulation through a unified perception-planning framework, outperforming modular pipeline-based methods.

Abstract: Planning and acting in 3D environments is a fundamental capability for robotic manipulation in the real world. Although prior work has explored predictive flow planners to guide 3D manipulation, existing approaches often rely on modular pipelines stacking multiple submodels, resulting in high computational overhead and limited real-time performance. To address these challenges, we introduce RoboFlow4D, a lightweight flow world model that unifies perception and planning by estimating temporal motion in physical 3D space. As an end-to-end framework, RoboFlow4D directly predicts multi-frame 3D flows from visual observations and textual instructions, providing explicit flow-based planning to guide action generation. This design allows seamless integration with general action policies, forming an efficient observation-planning-execution closed loop. Through slow-fast collaboration between flow prediction and action control, RoboFlow4D enables real-time and resource-efficient manipulation. Extensive experiments in both simulation and real-world settings demonstrate that RoboFlow4D consistently improves manipulation success rates and computational efficiency, advancing flow-guided planning for embodied intelligence.

</details>


### [171] [Visual Sculpting: Visually-Aligned Planning Representations for Long-Horizon Robot Clay Sculpting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17556)
*Peter Schaldenbrand,Jean Oh*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents a dynamics modeling and planning method for robotic clay sculpting using visually-aligned representations that capture lighting and texture features, enabling long-horizon (>100 actions) manipulation of deformable materials with performance comparable to state-of-the-art while compatible with visual planning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clay sculpting requires nuanced dexterous manipulation with long-horizon planning. Prior deformable object manipulation work either requires retraining a policy per goal or relies on sparse point cloud representations that fail to capture important clay features like textures.

Method: The method models dynamics of deformable materials and plans for robotic sculpting using a visually-aligned representation that captures lighting and texture features. Actions are represented as parametrized pushes into clay with a single end-effector.

Result: The dynamics model achieves comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods across three different deformable materials and various end-effectors. The approach is suitable for long-horizon (>100 actions) clay relief sculptures. The benefits of visually-aligned planning are demonstrated, along with analysis of why this representation is challenging compared to 3D representations.

Conclusion: Visually-aligned representations are beneficial for robotic clay sculpting but present planning challenges compared to 3D representations; the method enables long-horizon deformable object manipulation without per-goal retraining.

Abstract: Clay sculpting is a nuanced, artistic task involving dexterous manipulation with long-horizon planning to achieve high-level goals. As a robotics problem, we formulate clay sculpting as a shape-to-shape matching challenge. Prior deformable object manipulation work either requires retraining a policy per goal or relies on dynamics models which represent state as sparse point clouds which do not capture important clay features, such as textures, well. We present a method for modeling the dynamics of deformable materials and planning for robotic sculpting in a representation that is visually-aligned, capturing lighting and texture features. With three different deformable materials and various end-effectors, we demonstrate that our dynamics model is comparable in performance to the state-of-the-art with the added benefit of being compatible with visual planning. Our actions are represented as parametrized pushes into clay with a single end-effector, which proved to be suitable for long-horizon (>100 actions) clay relief sculptures. Lastly, we show the benefits of planning in a visually-aligned representation, but also provide analysis providing evidence as to why this representation is challenging to plan in compared to 3D representations.

</details>


### [172] [Motion-Uncertainty-Aware Next-Best-View Planning for Moving Object Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17593)
*Karen Li,Mattia Mantovani,Robert J. Wood,Lorenzo Sabattini,Stephanie Gil*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A motion-uncertainty-aware next-best-view framework for active 3D reconstruction of moving objects that accounts for object motion uncertainty by evaluating viewpoints over plausible future object states using Gaussian Process smoothing, achieving better reconstruction completeness than non-predictive NBV and tracking-only methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing NBV planners assume static objects, while motion-aware active perception prioritizes tracking/visibility over reconstruction coverage. No prior work jointly addresses informative view selection and motion uncertainty for reconstructing moving objects.

Method: Uses a fixed-lag Gaussian Process smoother to estimate and predict object state from noisy planar position measurements; generates candidate viewpoints around predicted object location; filters by reachability; and scores them by expected coverage over plausible future object states induced by motion and measurement uncertainty.

Result: Simulation and real-world experiments show improved reconstruction completeness compared to non-predictive NBV and prediction-only tracking methods.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively bridges coverage-driven active reconstruction and prediction-driven tracking, demonstrating that accounting for motion uncertainty during viewpoint selection improves reconstruction of moving objects.

Abstract: Active 3D reconstruction of moving objects requires selecting informative viewpoints while accounting for object motion uncertainty during the decision-to-execution delay. Existing methods address only parts of this problem: next-best-view (NBV) planners for object reconstruction typically optimize surface coverage but assume static objects, while motion-aware active perception for moving targets accounts for target motion but prioritizes tracking or visibility over reconstruction coverage. This work presents a motion-uncertainty-aware NBV framework for reconstructing an unknown rigid object undergoing planar motion, using noisy planar position measurements of the object and depth observations from a mobile robot. The key idea is to evaluate each candidate viewpoint by its expected observation quality over plausible future object states induced by motion and measurement uncertainty, rather than at a single predicted object pose. To obtain this predictive belief, a fixed-lag Gaussian Process smoother estimates and predicts the object state from noisy position measurements. The resulting belief is used to generate candidate viewpoints around the predicted object location, filter them by reachability, and estimate their expected coverage-driven scores. Simulation and real-world experiments demonstrate improved reconstruction completeness over non-predictive NBV and prediction-only tracking methods, bridging coverage-driven active reconstruction and prediction-driven tracking.

</details>


### [173] [From a Single Demonstration to a General Policy for Contact-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17601)
*Xing Li,Oliver Brock*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A Learning from Demonstration framework using environmental constraints as inductive bias achieves one-shot generalization in multi-stage contact-rich manipulation tasks, validated with over 90% success across seven real-world tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable one-shot generalization in multi-stage contact-rich manipulation tasks by leveraging environmental constraints as inductive bias, overcoming the limitations of trajectory-mimicking approaches that fail to generalize across varying object poses, geometries, and contact dynamics.

Method: A four-stage pipeline: (1) abstract a single demonstration into environmental-constraint primitives, (2) disambiguate primitives through self-guided exploration, (3) assimilate targeted human corrections for out-of-distribution variations, and (4) recover abstracted details online via compliant interaction.

Result: Achieved over 90% success rate across seven real-world multi-stage contact-rich manipulation tasks, demonstrating generalization across object poses, local geometries, and unmodeled contact dynamics.

Conclusion: Environmental constraints serve as fundamental building blocks for efficient generalization in LfD, enabling one-shot learning that generalizes by following constraints rather than mimicking trajectories.

Abstract: We present a Learning from Demonstration (LfD) framework that achieves one-shot generalization in multi-stage, contact-rich manipulation tasks. Central to our approach is the utilization of environmental constraints as the inductive bias. By representing a demonstration as a sequence of behaviors that exploit environmental constraints, the robot separates task-general structure -- the constraint types and their transitions -- from instance-specific details such as exact demonstration trajectories, poses, and local geometries. Our four-stage pipeline builds a complete policy on this representation: the robot first abstracts a single demonstration into environmental-constraint primitives, then disambiguates them through self-guided exploration, next assimilates targeted human corrections that handle out-of-distribution variations, and finally recovers the abstracted-away details online through compliant interaction. Because the resulting policy follows constraints rather than mimics trajectories, it generalizes across object poses, local geometries, and unmodeled contact dynamics. We validate our approach on seven real-world multi-stage contact-rich manipulation tasks and achieve over 90% success. These extensive experimental results establish environmental constraints as fundamental building blocks for efficient generalization in learning from demonstration.

</details>


### [174] [Mono-Hydra++: Real-Time Monocular Scene Graph Construction with Multi-Task Learning for 3D Indoor Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17661)
*U. V. B. L. Udugama,George Vosselman,Francesco Nex*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Mono-Hydra++ is a real-time monocular RGB+IMU pipeline for indoor metric semantic mapping and hierarchical 3D scene graph construction, achieving competitive accuracy against RGB-D baselines while eliminating the need for active depth sensors, and running embedded at 25.53 FPS on a Jetson Orin NX 16GB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional metric maps support localization and collision avoidance but lack semantic and relational structure needed for autonomous agile robots. Building 3D scene graphs on aerial/lightweight robots is difficult due to strict payload, power, and compute limits that make RGB-D and LiDAR sensors impractical.

Method: Mono-Hydra++ combines M2H-MX (a DINOv3-based multi-task model for depth and semantics) with a deep feature visual-inertial odometry front-end, sparse predicted depth constraints in VIO pose graph, semantic masking for dynamic regions, and pose-aware temporal alignment before volumetric fusion in the Mono-Hydra backend.

Result: On Go-SLAM ScanNet, Mono-Hydra++ achieves 1.6% lower average trajectory error than the strongest RGB-D baseline. On calibrated 7-Scenes, it improves average ATE by 29.8% over the strongest competing calibrated baseline. Deployed on Jetson Orin NX 16GB at 25.53 FPS using ONNX/TensorRT FP16.

Conclusion: Mono-Hydra++ provides real-time metric semantic mapping and scene graph construction for resource-constrained robotic platforms without relying on active depth sensors.

Abstract: Autonomous agile robots need more than metric geometry: they must understand objects, rooms, places, and spatial relations for search, inspection, exploration, and human robot interaction. Conventional metric maps support localization and collision avoidance, but do not provide this semantic and relational structure. 3D scene graphs address this gap by connecting geometry with object level and room level understanding. Building such representations on agile platforms remains difficult because aerial and lightweight robots operate under strict payload, power, and compute limits, making RGB-D cameras and LiDAR sensors impractical for many onboard settings. We present Mono-Hydra++, a real time monocular RGB plus IMU pipeline for indoor metric semantic mapping and hierarchical 3D scene graph construction. The system combines M2H-MX, a DINOv3 based multi-task model for depth and semantics, with a deep feature visual inertial odometry front end, sparse predicted depth constraints in the VIO derived pose graph, semantic masking for dynamic regions, and pose aware temporal alignment before volumetric fusion in the Mono-Hydra backend. On the Go-SLAM ScanNet evaluation subset, Mono-Hydra++ achieves 1.6% lower average trajectory error than the strongest RGB-D baseline in our comparison, while using only monocular RGB plus IMU input. On calibrated 7-Scenes, it improves average ATE by 29.8% over the strongest competing calibrated baseline. We further validate Mono-Hydra++ in a real ITC building deployment using RealSense RGB plus IMU and demonstrate embedded feasibility by deploying the ONNX/TensorRT FP16 M2H-MX-L perception model at 25.53 FPS on a Jetson Orin NX 16GB. These results show that Mono-Hydra++ can provide real time metric semantic mapping and scene graph construction for resource constrained robotic platforms without relying on active depth sensors.

</details>


### [175] [PRIME: Physically-consistent Robotic Inertial and Motion Estimation for Legged and Humanoid Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17681)
*Jiarong Kang,Kunzhao Ren,Tao Pang,Xiaobin Xiong*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: PRIME is a MAP-based framework that refines kinematics and actuator commands into dynamically consistent trajectories by jointly estimating contact forces and inertial parameters using differentiable contact dynamics with complementarity constraints, improving motion estimation on quadrupedal and humanoid robots.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard sensing pipelines (EKFs, motion capture) recover only kinematics, ignoring contact forces, timing, and inertial parameters, leading to kinematic reconstructions that violate rigid-body dynamics during contact-rich motions.

Method: PRIME formulates a Maximum A Posteriori (MAP) optimization that refines onboard kinematics and actuator commands into dynamically consistent trajectories, jointly estimating frictional contact forces and inertial parameters using differentiable contact dynamics with smoothed complementarity constraints and an Anitescu-style friction model.

Result: Evaluated on contact-rich locomotion with quadrupedal robots and Unitree G1 humanoid, showing improved trajectory consistency and accurate inertial parameter identification.

Conclusion: PRIME improves state estimation and feedback control with calibrated inertial parameters, and provides force- and contact-annotated motion reconstructions for downstream learning applications such as behavior modeling and robot foundation models.

Abstract: Humanoid and legged robots interact with the environment through intermittent contacts, making accurate motion estimation fundamentally dependent on reasoning about contact dynamics. However, standard sensing pipelines-whether based on onboard proprioception with Extended Kalman Filters (EKFs) or external motion capture systems-recover only kinematics, while contact forces, contact timing, and inertial parameters remain unobserved. As a result, purely kinematic reconstructions often violate rigid-body dynamics, particularly during contact-rich motions. To enable accurate motion estimation from onboard kinematics in real-world deployment, we propose PRIME (Physically-consistent Robotic Inertial and Motion Estimation), a Maximum A Posteriori (MAP) formulation that refines measured kinematics and actuator commands into a dynamically consistent trajectory while jointly estimating frictional contact forces and physically consistent inertial parameters. Our approach incorporates differentiable contact dynamics with smoothed complementarity constraints and an Anitescu-style friction model, yielding a smooth optimization problem that remains tractable across versatile contact transitions. We evaluate PRIME on contact-rich locomotion with quadrupedal robots and the Unitree G1 humanoid, demonstrating improved trajectory consistency and accurate inertial parameter identification. Beyond improving state estimation and feedback control with calibrated inertial parameters, PRIME produces force- and contact-annotated motion reconstructions from real robots in deployment, which can be used to provide high-quality data for downstream learning applications, including large-scale behavior modeling and robot foundation models.

</details>


### [176] [CosFly-Track: A Large-Scale Multi-Modal Dataset for UAV Visual Tracking via Multi-Constraint Trajectory Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17776)
*Xiangyue Wang,Hanxuan Chen,Songsheng Cheng,Ruilong Ren,Jie Zheng,Shuai Yuan,Tianle Zeng,Hanzhong Guo,Kangli Wang,Ji Pei*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: CosFlyTrack is a large-scale multi-modal dataset (2.4M timesteps, 7 channels) for UAV visual tracking in urban environments, generated from 6,000 pedestrian paths via a novel multi-constraint optimizer (MuCO) that plans continuous 3D trajectories with visibility, collision, and kinematic constraints. Fine-tuning seven VLMs on this dataset improves tracking success (SR@1m) from near-zero to 78.3-95.6%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing aerial VLN datasets focus on goal-oriented navigation to static destinations; there is no dedicated large-scale dataset for UAV visual tracking of dynamic targets (continuously following a moving subject while maintaining visibility).

Method: A scalable generation pipeline producing ~12,000 UAV trajectories from 6,000 pedestrian paths, with seven aligned data channels. Expert trajectories are generated by MuCO, a multi-constraint optimizer that plans in continuous 3D space using BVH-accelerated intersection tests, jointly optimizing target visibility, viewpoint quality, collision avoidance, smoothness, and kinematic feasibility.

Result: Fine-tuning seven VLMs on CosFlyTrack yields 78.3-95.6% SR@1m, a 53-69 percentage point improvement over zero-shot baselines.

Conclusion: CosFlyTrack serves as an effective training resource for dynamic target-following agents, filling a critical gap in aerial VLN datasets.

Abstract: Recent aerial vision-language navigation (VLN) datasets have grown rapidly, but they primarily address goal-oriented navigation to static destinations, leaving UAV visual tracking -- continuously following a moving target while maintaining visibility -- largely without dedicated training data. We introduce CosFlyTrack, a large-scale multi-modal dataset and scalable generation pipeline for UAV visual tracking in urban environments. The dataset provides approximately 12,000 expert and perturbed UAV trajectories generated from 6,000 pedestrian paths, comprising 2.4 million timesteps (approximately 334 hours) with seven aligned data channels: RGB, metric depth, semantic segmentation, six-degree-of-freedom drone pose, target state with visibility flag, bilingual (Chinese-English) instructions, and trajectory-pair metadata. To generate high-quality expert trajectories, we develop MuCO, a multi-constraint optimizer that plans directly in continuous three-dimensional space with BVH-accelerated collision and visibility queries, jointly enforcing target visibility, viewpoint quality, collision avoidance, smoothness, and kinematic feasibility, avoiding the discretization artifacts and post-hoc smoothing of grid-based planners. Fine-tuning experiments on seven vision-language models show that CosFlyTrack improves tracking performance to 78.3 to 95.6 percent SR@1 meter, a 53 to 69 percentage point gain over zero-shot baselines, supporting the dataset as a training resource for dynamic target-following agents. The dataset is publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/AutelRobotics/CosFly; evaluation scripts and pre-trained checkpoints are hosted at https://huggingface.co/AutelRobotics/CosFly-Track.

</details>


### [177] [Optimal Knock-Pick Planning for Tightly Packed Tabletop Blocks With Parallel Grippers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17800)
*Hao Lu,Rahul Shome*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper addresses the challenge of rearranging densely packed tabletop objects by introducing a directional knock primitive when parallel-gripper picks are infeasible. It formulates the optimal knock-pick problem for uniformly sized blocks on grid layouts, uses graphical abstractions with minimal constraining gadgets and maximum-weight perfect matching to compute optimal plans in polynomial time, and validates the approach through synthetic experiments and IsaacSim simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Parallel-gripper picking becomes infeasible when objects are densely packed without sufficient clearance, yet practical tabletop rearrangement tasks often involve such tight configurations. The work aims to systematically handle these scenarios by combining prehensile and non-prehensile actions.

Method: The authors introduce a directional knock primitive and formulate the optimal knock-pick problem. They propose abstractions covering minimal constraining gadgets to identify necessary knocks, then apply maximum-weight perfect matching on a graphical abstraction to compute optimal plans (minimizing actions) in polynomial time.

Result: Experiments across increasing grid sizes in synthetic settings and IsaacSim demonstrate efficient computation of optimal plans, validating the theoretical framework.

Conclusion: The theoretical observations serve as a promising foundation for rigorously building efficient manipulation strategies that interleave prehensile and non-prehensile actions for dense object rearrangement.

Abstract: Rearranging densely packed tabletop objects is challenging when parallel-gripper picks are infeasible without sufficient clearance around an object. This work studies the problem characteristics for practically motivated settings with uniformly sized blocks placed at planar tabletop grid locations. Since purely prehensile removal can become infeasible, a directional knock primitive is therefore introduced and the optimal knock-pick variant of the problem is formulated. The work proposes a series of abstractions wherein minimal constraining gadgets are covered to identify the necessary knocks. Utilizing a maximum-weight perfect matching on a graphical abstraction yields efficient polynomial-time computation of the optimal plan that minimizes the number of actions. Experiments are reported for increasing grid sizes in synthetic settings as well as in IsaacSim. The theoretical observations provide a promising stepping stone towards rigorously building efficient manipulation strategies that interleave prehensile and non-prehensile actions.

</details>


### [178] [Virtues of Ordered Chaos: Planning with Topple Actions in Tabletop Stack Rearrangement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17815)
*Hao Lu,Rahul Shome*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper introduces toppling as an efficient nonprehensile aggregating action for stack rearrangement in tabletop settings, modeling it via a directed graphical abstraction akin to a pebble motion problem to interleave topple with pick-and-place actions, achieving faster execution in IsaacSim simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficient object manipulation strategies are critical for automation; this work aims to compress long sequences of intermediate relocations in stack rearrangement by augmenting task planning with richer nonprehensile aggregating actions (toppling).

Method: A novel aggregating gadget for topple is introduced as a directed graphical abstraction, transforming candidate task plan computation into a variant of the pebble motion problem where objects are treated as pebbles, interleaving pick-and-place with topple actions.

Result: Benchmarks in IsaacSim-based physics simulation show clear benefits of faster execution compared to using only pick-and-place actions.

Conclusion: Although focused on topple, similar abstractions can model other aggregating actions (e.g., scoop), providing strong preliminary evidence of the benefits of abstractions for rich object interactions in manipulation.

Abstract: Efficient object manipulation strategies have significant impact in automation applications. In this work, the stack rearrangement in tabletop settings is studied, with a focus on augmenting the task planning domain with richer nonprehensile aggregating actions, in particular the toppling of objects from a stack to the table. Toppling can compress long sequences of intermediate relocations. Computed plans need to interleave pick-and-place actions with topple throughout its plan based on the problem. In order to generate the task plan and model an abstraction to compute solutions that include both pick-and-place and topple actions, a novel aggregating gadget for topple is introduced. Using this directed graphical abstraction, candidate task plan computation becomes a variant of the pebble motion problem, treating objects as pebbles. Benchmarks are then reported in a IsaacSim-based physics simulation. Results highlight clear benefits of achieving faster execution than solely using pick-and-place actions. Though this work primarily investigates the topple action, we demonstrate that similar abstractions can model other aggregating actions of interest, like scoop. The current work provides a preliminary, strong indication of the promising benefits of abstractions for rich object interactions in manipulation applications.

</details>


### [179] [A Dexterous and Compliant Gripper With Soft Hydraulic Actuation for Microgravity Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17851)
*William Su,Jordan Kam,Yixiao Wang,Jianshu Zhou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents DexCoHand, a dexterous and compliant 6-DOF two-finger gripper integrated with Astrobee free-flying robot for microgravity manipulation, which reduces unintended base motion during perching and manipulation tasks compared to the existing 1-DOF claw gripper.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Astrobee's existing 1-DOF underactuated compliant claw gripper enables perching but provides limited continuous dexterous manipulation, and contact forces from manipulation directly couple into base motion of the free-flying robot.

Method: The authors integrate DexCoHand (a dexterous, compliant two-finger 6-DOF gripper) with Astrobee, and evaluate it in MuJoCo simulation using Astrobee's standard handrail perching sequence (approach, perching, pan and tilt motions), along with hardware experiments on Earth.

Result: DexCoHand preserves commanded pan and tilt motions while reducing unintended cross-axis base motion compared to Astrobee's existing gripper. Hardware experiments demonstrate dexterous manipulation capabilities.

Conclusion: DexCoHand improves microgravity manipulation by reducing disturbance to the free-flying base while enabling more dexterous manipulation, showing potential for adaptable intelligent manipulation tasks in space.

Abstract: Astrobee's existing one-degree-of-freedom (DOF) underactuated compliant claw gripper enables perching on the International Space Station (ISS), but provides limited capability for continuous dexterous manipulation. More complex microgravity tasks require an end-effector that can maintain stable contact while limiting disturbance to the free-flying base, since contact forces directly couple into base motion. This article presents the integration of DexCoHand, a dexterous and compliant two-finger, 6-DOF gripper, with the Astrobee free-flying robot for microgravity manipulation. The system is evaluated in MuJoCo using Astrobee's standard handrail perching sequence, including approach, perching, and subsequent pan and tilt motions. Compared with Astrobee's existing gripper, DexCoHand preserves the commanded pan and tilt motions while reducing unintended cross-axis base motion. Hardware experiments on Earth further demonstrate DexCoHand's dexterous manipulation capabilities and its potential for more adaptable intelligent manipulation tasks.

</details>


### [180] [WorldArena 2.0: Extending Embodied World Model Benchmarking on Modality, Functionality and Platform](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17912)
*Yu Shang,Yinzhou Tang,Yiding Ma,Zhuohang Li,Lei Jin,Weikang Su,Xin Jin,Zhaolu Wang,Ziyou Wang,Xin Zhang,Haisheng Su,Weizhen He,Wei Wu,Haoyi Duan,Gordon Wetzstein,Xihui Liu,Dhruv Shah,Zhaoxiang Zhang,Zhibo Chen,Jun Zhu,Yonghong Tian,Tat-Seng Chua,Wenwu Zhu,Chen Gao,Yong Li*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: WorldArena 2.0 is an expanded benchmark for embodied world models that evaluates along three dimensions: modality (vision→visuotactile), functionality (policy evaluation→interactive RL environments), and platform (simulator→real-world robots), assessing perceptual quality, interactive utility, and cross-platform performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing embodied world model benchmarks are limited to vision-only prediction, offline embodied applications, and simulator-based evaluation, which is insufficient for assessing increasingly comprehensive world models.

Method: WorldArena 2.0 systematically broadens evaluation along three dimensions: (1) modality: from vision-only to visuotactile; (2) functionality: from policy evaluation/planning to using world models as interactive RL environments for policy optimization; (3) platform: from simulator-only to a suite of simulated and real-world robotic settings across multiple embodiments.

Result: A comprehensive benchmark that evaluates perceptual quality, interactive utility, and cross-platform performance under a standardized protocol across diverse modalities, functionalities, and robotic platforms.

Conclusion: WorldArena 2.0 provides a comprehensive testbed for tracking progress toward embodied world models by expanding evaluation across modality, functionality, and platform dimensions.

Abstract: World models have emerged as a central paradigm for embodied intelligence, enabling agents to predict action-conditioned future and reason about environmental dynamics. However, existing embodied world model benchmarks are still largely confined to vision-only prediction, offline embodied applications, and simulator-based evaluation, making them insufficient for assessing increasingly comprehensive world models.
